           │ > Consider a system essentially consisting of a sender S and │
           │ a receiver R that communicate with each other over channels  │
           │ c and d. The receiver sends messages to the sender via       │
           │ channel d, and the sender sends messages to the receiver via │
           │ channel c. It is assumed that both channels have an          │
           │ unlimited buffer. Channel c is unreliable in the sense that  │
           │ data may get lost when being transmitted from the sender S   │
           │ to channel c. Once messages are stored in the buffer of      │
           │ channel c, they are neither corrupted nor lost. Channel d is │
           │ assumed to be perfect. The goal is to design a communication │
           │ protocol that ensures any distinct transmitted datum by S to │
           │ be delivered to R. To ensure this in the presence of         │
           │ possible message losses, sender S resorts to                 │
           │ retransmissions. Messages are transmitted one by one, i.e.,  │
           │ S starts sending a new message once the transmission of the  │
           │ previous message has been successful. This is a simple flow  │
           │ control principle, known as "send-and-wait". Each message m  │
           │ from S is sent with a control bit b, and when R receives     │
           │ this, it sends an acknowledgement consisting of the control  │
           │ bit it just received. On receipt of the acknowledgement, S   │
           │ sends a new message with the negation of the control bit. If │
           │ S has to wait too long for the acknowledgement, it times out │
           │ and retransmits the original message and control bit. Build  │
           │ a model of this system, using submodules for S and R. Use    │
           │ the variable names c, d, S, R, m and b.                      │
           │                                                              │
           │ Reply with your Python code inside one unique code block.    │

           │                                                              │
           │ > Consider a system essentially consisting of a sender S and │
           │ a receiver R that communicate with each other over channels  │
           │ c and d. The receiver sends messages to the sender via       │
           │ channel d, and the sender sends messages to the receiver via │
           │ channel c. It is assumed that both channels have an          │
           │ unlimited buffer. Channel c is unreliable in the sense that  │
           │ data may get lost when being transmitted from the sender S   │
           │ to channel c. Once messages are stored in the buffer of      │
           │ channel c, they are neither corrupted nor lost. Channel d is │
           │ assumed to be perfect. The goal is to design a communication │
           │ protocol that ensures any distinct transmitted datum by S to │
           │ be delivered to R. To ensure this in the presence of         │
           │ possible message losses, sender S resorts to                 │
           │ retransmissions. Messages are transmitted one by one, i.e.,  │
           │ S starts sending a new message once the transmission of the  │
           │ previous message has been successful. This is a simple flow  │
           │ control principle, known as "send-and-wait". Each message m  │
           │ from S is sent with a control bit b, and when R receives     │
           │ this, it sends an acknowledgement consisting of the control  │
           │ bit it just received. On receipt of the acknowledgement, S   │
           │ sends a new message with the negation of the control bit. If │
           │ S has to wait too long for the acknowledgement, it times out │
           │ and retransmits the original message and control bit. Build  │
           │ a model of this system, using submodules for S and R. Use    │
           │ the variable names c, d, S, R, m and b.                      │
           │                                                              │
           │ Reply with your Python code inside one unique code block.    │
           │                                                              │
           │ ```python                                                    │
           │ class Module:                                                │
           │     """An abstract class to represent a UCLID5 module."""    │
           │                                                              │
           │     def types(self):                                         │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the type declarations.         │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines a  │
           │ 8-bit type called T:                                         │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def types(self):                                     │
           │             self.T = BitVector(8)                            │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def locals(self):                                        │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the local variables and their  │
           │ types.                                                       │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines an │
           │ 8-bit variable x                                             │
           │         and an integer variable y:                           │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def locals(self):                                    │
           │             self.x = BitVector(8)                            │
           │             self.y = Integer()                               │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def inputs(self):                                        │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the input variables and their  │
           │ types.                                                       │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines an │
           │ input variable x,                                            │
           │         which is an array of 8-bit bitvectors indexed by     │
           │ 2-bit bitvectors:                                            │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def inputs(self):                                    │
           │             self.x = Array(BitVector(2), BitVector(8))       │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def outputs(self):                                       │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the output variables and their │
           │ types.                                                       │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines an │
           │ output variable y,                                           │
           │         which is a real number:                              │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def outputs(self):                                   │
           │             self.y = Real()                                  │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def shared_vars(self):                                   │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the shared variables and their │
           │ types.                                                       │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines a  │
           │ shared variable z,                                           │
           │         which is an array of booleans indexed by integers:   │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def shared_vars(self):                               │
           │             self.z = Array(Integer(), Boolean())             │
           │         ```                                                  │

           │                                                              │
           │ > Consider a system essentially consisting of a sender S and │
           │ a receiver R that communicate with each other over channels  │
           │ c and d. The receiver sends messages to the sender via       │
           │ channel d, and the sender sends messages to the receiver via │
           │ channel c. It is assumed that both channels have an          │
           │ unlimited buffer. Channel c is unreliable in the sense that  │
           │ data may get lost when being transmitted from the sender S   │
           │ to channel c. Once messages are stored in the buffer of      │
           │ channel c, they are neither corrupted nor lost. Channel d is │
           │ assumed to be perfect. The goal is to design a communication │
           │ protocol that ensures any distinct transmitted datum by S to │
           │ be delivered to R. To ensure this in the presence of         │
           │ possible message losses, sender S resorts to                 │
           │ retransmissions. Messages are transmitted one by one, i.e.,  │
           │ S starts sending a new message once the transmission of the  │
           │ previous message has been successful. This is a simple flow  │
           │ control principle, known as "send-and-wait". Each message m  │
           │ from S is sent with a control bit b, and when R receives     │
           │ this, it sends an acknowledgement consisting of the control  │
           │ bit it just received. On receipt of the acknowledgement, S   │
           │ sends a new message with the negation of the control bit. If │
           │ S has to wait too long for the acknowledgement, it times out │
           │ and retransmits the original message and control bit. Build  │
           │ a model of this system, using submodules for S and R. Use    │
           │ the variable names c, d, S, R, m and b.                      │
           │                                                              │
           │ Reply with your Python code inside one unique code block.    │
           │                                                              │
           │ ```python                                                    │
           │ class Module:                                                │
           │     """An abstract class to represent a UCLID5 module."""    │
           │                                                              │
           │     def types(self):                                         │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the type declarations.         │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines a  │
           │ 8-bit type called T:                                         │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def types(self):                                     │
           │             self.T = BitVector(8)                            │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def locals(self):                                        │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the local variables and their  │
           │ types.                                                       │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines an │
           │ 8-bit variable x                                             │
           │         and an integer variable y:                           │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def locals(self):                                    │
           │             self.x = BitVector(8)                            │
           │             self.y = Integer()                               │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def inputs(self):                                        │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the input variables and their  │
           │ types.                                                       │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines an │
           │ input variable x,                                            │
           │         which is an array of 8-bit bitvectors indexed by     │
           │ 2-bit bitvectors:                                            │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def inputs(self):                                    │
           │             self.x = Array(BitVector(2), BitVector(8))       │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def outputs(self):                                       │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the output variables and their │
           │ types.                                                       │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines an │
           │ output variable y,                                           │
           │         which is a real number:                              │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def outputs(self):                                   │
           │             self.y = Real()                                  │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def shared_vars(self):                                   │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the shared variables and their │
           │ types.                                                       │

           │                                                              │
           │ > Consider a system essentially consisting of a sender S and │
           │ a receiver R that communicate with each other over channels  │
           │ c and d. The receiver sends messages to the sender via       │
           │ channel d, and the sender sends messages to the receiver via │
           │ channel c. It is assumed that both channels have an          │
           │ unlimited buffer. Channel c is unreliable in the sense that  │
           │ data may get lost when being transmitted from the sender S   │
           │ to channel c. Once messages are stored in the buffer of      │
           │ channel c, they are neither corrupted nor lost. Channel d is │
           │ assumed to be perfect. The goal is to design a communication │
           │ protocol that ensures any distinct transmitted datum by S to │
           │ be delivered to R. To ensure this in the presence of         │
           │ possible message losses, sender S resorts to                 │
           │ retransmissions. Messages are transmitted one by one, i.e.,  │
           │ S starts sending a new message once the transmission of the  │
           │ previous message has been successful. This is a simple flow  │
           │ control principle, known as "send-and-wait". Each message m  │
           │ from S is sent with a control bit b, and when R receives     │
           │ this, it sends an acknowledgement consisting of the control  │
           │ bit it just received. On receipt of the acknowledgement, S   │
           │ sends a new message with the negation of the control bit. If │
           │ S has to wait too long for the acknowledgement, it times out │
           │ and retransmits the original message and control bit. Build  │
           │ a model of this system, using submodules for S and R. Use    │
           │ the variable names c, d, S, R, m and b.                      │
           │                                                              │
           │ Reply with your Python code inside one unique code block.    │
           │                                                              │
           │ ```python                                                    │
           │ class Module:                                                │
           │     """An abstract class to represent a UCLID5 module."""    │
           │                                                              │
           │     def types(self):                                         │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the type declarations.         │

           │ Write Python code that extends the `Module` class below to   │
           │ complete the following task.                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │ > Consider a system essentially consisting of a sender S and │
           │ a receiver R that communicate with each other over channels  │
           │ c and d. The receiver sends messages to the sender via       │
           │ channel d, and the sender sends messages to the receiver via │
           │ channel c. It is assumed that both channels have an          │
           │ unlimited buffer. Channel c is unreliable in the sense that  │
           │ data may get lost when being transmitted from the sender S   │
           │ to channel c. Once messages are stored in the buffer of      │
           │ channel c, they are neither corrupted nor lost. Channel d is │
           │ assumed to be perfect. The goal is to design a communication │
           │ protocol that ensures any distinct transmitted datum by S to │
           │ be delivered to R. To ensure this in the presence of         │
           │ possible message losses, sender S resorts to                 │
           │ retransmissions. Messages are transmitted one by one, i.e.,  │
           │ S starts sending a new message once the transmission of the  │
           │ previous message has been successful. This is a simple flow  │
           │ control principle, known as "send-and-wait". Each message m  │
           │ from S is sent with a control bit b, and when R receives     │
           │ this, it sends an acknowledgement consisting of the control  │
           │ bit it just received. On receipt of the acknowledgement, S   │
           │ sends a new message with the negation of the control bit. If │
           │ S has to wait too long for the acknowledgement, it times out │
           │ and retransmits the original message and control bit. Build  │
           │ a model of this system, using submodules for S and R. Use    │
           │ the variable names c, d, S, R, m and b.                      │
           │                                                              │
           │ Reply with your Python code inside one unique code block.    │

           │                                                              │
           │ > Consider a system essentially consisting of a sender S and │
           │ a receiver R that communicate with each other over channels  │
           │ c and d. The receiver sends messages to the sender via       │
           │ channel d, and the sender sends messages to the receiver via │
           │ channel c. It is assumed that both channels have an          │
           │ unlimited buffer. Channel c is unreliable in the sense that  │
           │ data may get lost when being transmitted from the sender S   │
           │ to channel c. Once messages are stored in the buffer of      │
           │ channel c, they are neither corrupted nor lost. Channel d is │
           │ assumed to be perfect. The goal is to design a communication │
           │ protocol that ensures any distinct transmitted datum by S to │
           │ be delivered to R. To ensure this in the presence of         │
           │ possible message losses, sender S resorts to                 │
           │ retransmissions. Messages are transmitted one by one, i.e.,  │
           │ S starts sending a new message once the transmission of the  │
           │ previous message has been successful. This is a simple flow  │
           │ control principle, known as "send-and-wait". Each message m  │
           │ from S is sent with a control bit b, and when R receives     │
           │ this, it sends an acknowledgement consisting of the control  │
           │ bit it just received. On receipt of the acknowledgement, S   │
           │ sends a new message with the negation of the control bit. If │
           │ S has to wait too long for the acknowledgement, it times out │
           │ and retransmits the original message and control bit. Build  │
           │ a model of this system, using submodules for S and R. Use    │
           │ the variable names c, d, S, R, m and b.                      │
           │                                                              │

           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │

           │                                                              │
           │ > Consider a system essentially consisting of a sender S and │
           │ a receiver R that communicate with each other over channels  │
           │ c and d. The receiver sends messages to the sender via       │
           │ channel d, and the sender sends messages to the receiver via │
           │ channel c. It is assumed that both channels have an          │
           │ unlimited buffer. Channel c is unreliable in the sense that  │
           │ data may get lost when being transmitted from the sender S   │
           │ to channel c. Once messages are stored in the buffer of      │
           │ channel c, they are neither corrupted nor lost. Channel d is │
           │ assumed to be perfect. The goal is to design a communication │
           │ protocol that ensures any distinct transmitted datum by S to │
           │ be delivered to R. To ensure this in the presence of         │
           │ possible message losses, sender S resorts to                 │
           │ retransmissions. Messages are transmitted one by one, i.e.,  │
           │ S starts sending a new message once the transmission of the  │
           │ previous message has been successful. This is a simple flow  │
           │ control principle, known as "send-and-wait". Each message m  │
           │ from S is sent with a control bit b, and when R receives     │
           │ this, it sends an acknowledgement consisting of the control  │
           │ bit it just received. On receipt of the acknowledgement, S   │
           │ sends a new message with the negation of the control bit. If │
           │ S has to wait too long for the acknowledgement, it times out │
           │ and retransmits the original message and control bit. Build  │
           │ a model of this system, using submodules for S and R. Use    │
           │ the variable names c, d, S, R, m and b.                      │

           │ Consider a system essentially consisting of a sender S and a │

           │                                                              │
           │ It is assumed that both channels have an unlimited buffer.   │
           │ Channel c is unreliable in the sense that data may get lost  │
           │ when being transmitted from the sender S to channel c. Once  │
           │ messages are stored in the buffer of channel c, they are     │
           │ neither corrupted nor lost. Channel d is assumed to be       │
           │ perfect.                                                     │
           │ The goal is to design a communication protocol that ensures  │
           │ any distinct transmitted datum by S to be delivered to R.    │
           │ To ensure this in the presence of possible message losses,   │
           │ sender S resorts to retransmissions.                         │
           │                                                              │
           │ Messages are transmitted one by one, i.e., S starts sending  │
           │ a new message once the transmission of the previous message  │
           │ has been successful. This is a simple flow control           │
           │ principle, known as "send-and-wait".                         │
           │                                                              │
           │ Each message m from S is sent with a control bit b, and when │
           │ R receives this, it sends an acknowledgement consisting of   │
           │ the control bit it just received. On receipt of the          │
           │ acknowledgement, S sends a new message with the negation of  │
           │ the control bit.  If S has to wait too long for the          │
           │ acknowledgement, it times out and retransmits the original   │
           │ message and control bit.                                     │
           │                                                              │
           │ Build a model of this system, using submodules for S and R.  │
           │                                                              │

           │ be delivered to R. To ensure this in the presence of         │
           │ possible message losses, sender S resorts to                 │
           │ retransmissions. Messages are transmitted one by one, i.e.,  │
           │ S starts sending a new message once the transmission of the  │
           │ previous message has been successful. This is a simple flow  │
           │ control principle, known as "send-and-wait". Each message m  │
           │ from S is sent with a control bit b, and when R receives     │
           │ this, it sends an acknowledgement consisting of the control  │
           │ bit it just received. On receipt of the acknowledgement, S   │
           │ sends a new message with the negation of the control bit. If │
           │ S has to wait too long for the acknowledgement, it times out │
           │ and retransmits the original message and control bit. Build  │
           │ a model of this system, using submodules for S and R. Use    │
           │ the variable names c, d, S, R, m and b.                      │

           │ class CommunicationProtocol(Module):                         │
           │     def types(self):                                         │
           │         self.Channel = Boolean                               │
           │         self.Variable = Boolean                              │
           │                                                              │
           │     def locals(self):                                        │
           │         self.c = self.Channel()                              │
           │         self.d = self.Channel()                              │
           │         self.S = Boolean()                                   │
           │         self.R = Boolean()                                   │
           │         self.m = self.Variable()                             │
           │         self.b = Boolean()                                   │
           │                                                              │
           │     def next(self):                                          │
           │         if (self.c != self.d):                               │
           │             self.m = self.S                                  │
           │             self.b = Not(self.b)                             │
           │         if (self.R != self.S):                               │
           │             self.R = self.m                                  │
           │             self.b = Not(self.b)                             │
           │                                                              │
           │     def specification(self):                                 │
           │         self.c_inv = Forall(self.c0, Boolean, (self.c0 !=    │
           │ self.d))                                                     │
           │         self.d_inv = Forall(self.d0, Boolean,                │
           │ Implies((self.d0 == self.R), (self.m == self.R)))            │
           │         self.S_inv = Forall(self.S0, Boolean,                │
           │ Implies((self.S0 == self.m), (self.b != self.b)))            │
           │         self.R_inv = Forall(self.R0, Boolean,                │
           │ Implies((self.R0 == self.m), (self.b == self.b)))            │

           │ Implies((self.b0 != self.b), (self.R == self.m)))            │
           │         return (self.b_inv and (self.m_inv and (self.R_inv   │
           │ and (self.S_inv and (self.c_inv and self.d_inv))))           │

           │ c and d. The receiver sends messages to the sender via       │

           │ unlimited buffer. Channel c is unreliable in the sense that  │
           │ data may get lost when being transmitted from the sender S   │
           │ to channel c. Once messages are stored in the buffer of      │
           │ channel c, they are neither corrupted nor lost. Channel d is │

           │                                                              │
           │ > Consider a system essentially consisting of a sender S and │
           │ a receiver R that communicate with each other over channels  │
           │ c and d. The receiver sends messages to the sender via       │
           │ channel d, and the sender sends messages to the receiver via │
           │ channel c. It is assumed that both channels have an          │
           │ unlimited buffer. Channel c is unreliable in the sense that  │
           │ data may get lost when being transmitted from the sender S   │
           │ to channel c. Once messages are stored in the buffer of      │
           │ channel c, they are neither corrupted nor lost. Channel d is │
           │ assumed to be perfect. The goal is to design a communication │
           │ protocol that ensures any distinct transmitted datum by S to │
           │ be delivered to R. To ensure this in the presence of         │
           │ possible message losses, sender S resorts to                 │
           │ retransmissions. Messages are transmitted one by one, i.e.,  │
           │ S starts sending a new message once the transmission of the  │
           │ previous message has been successful. This is a simple flow  │
           │ control principle, known as "send-and-wait". Each message m  │
           │ from S is sent with a control bit b, and when R receives     │
           │ this, it sends an acknowledgement consisting of the control  │
           │ bit it just received. On receipt of the acknowledgement, S   │
           │ sends a new message with the negation of the control bit. If │
           │ S has to wait too long for the acknowledgement, it times out │
           │ and retransmits the original message and control bit. Build  │
           │ a model of this system, using submodules for S and R. Use    │
           │ the variable names c, d, S, R, m and b.                      │
           │                                                              │
           │ Reply with your Python code inside one unique code block.    │
           │                                                              │
           │ ```python                                                    │
           │ class Module:                                                │
           │     """An abstract class to represent a UCLID5 module."""    │
           │                                                              │
           │     def types(self):                                         │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the type declarations.         │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines a  │
           │ 8-bit type called T:                                         │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def types(self):                                     │
           │             self.T = BitVector(8)                            │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def locals(self):                                        │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the local variables and their  │
           │ types.                                                       │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines an │
           │ 8-bit variable x                                             │
           │         and an integer variable y:                           │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def locals(self):                                    │
           │             self.x = BitVector(8)                            │
           │             self.y = Integer()                               │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def inputs(self):                                        │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the input variables and their  │
           │ types.                                                       │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines an │
           │ input variable x,                                            │
           │         which is an array of 8-bit bitvectors indexed by     │
           │ 2-bit bitvectors:                                            │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def inputs(self):                                    │
           │             self.x = Array(BitVector(2), BitVector(8))       │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def outputs(self):                                       │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the output variables and their │
           │ types.                                                       │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines an │
           │ output variable y,                                           │
           │         which is a real number:                              │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def outputs(self):                                   │
           │             self.y = Real()                                  │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def shared_vars(self):                                   │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the shared variables and their │
           │ types.                                                       │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines a  │
           │ shared variable z,                                           │
           │         which is an array of booleans indexed by integers:   │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def shared_vars(self):                               │
           │             self.z = Array(Integer(), Boolean())             │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def instances(self):                                     │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the instances of other modules │
           │ and relates their                                            │
           │         input, output, and shared variables to local         │
           │ variables. Every instance                                    │
           │         variable must be related to a local variable. For    │
           │ example, let M be                                            │
           │         another module with inputs x and y, and output z.    │
           │ The following                                                │
           │         implementation defines an instance of M called m,    │
           │ and connects M's                                             │
           │         input variable x to the local variable self.a, M's   │
           │ input variable y to                                          │
           │         the local variable self.b, and M's output variable z │
           │ to the local                                                 │
           │         variable self.c:                                     │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def instances(self):                                 │
           │             self.m = M(x=self.a, y=self.b, z=self.c)         │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def init(self):                                          │
           │         """(Optional) Defines how variables are initialized. │
           │         For example, the following implementation            │
           │ initializes x to 0 if y is                                   │
           │         greater than or equal to 20:                         │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def init(self):                                      │

           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │

           │         """(Optional) Defines the transition relation.       │

           │ x or decrements y                                            │
           │         depending on z:                                      │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def next(self):                                      │
           │             if self.z:                                       │
           │                 self.x = self.x + 1                          │
           │             else:                                            │

           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def specification(self):                                 │
           │         """(Required) Defines the specification in terms of  │

           │                                                              │
           │         Returns:                                             │

           │ fix: The invariant S_inv in specification is incorrect. It   │

           │ received.                                                    │

           │ simultaneously as d is acknowledging, i.e., the messages     │
           │ should alternate between channels by their indices. Fix the  │

           │ simply inequality.                                           │

           │ to show R receives m only when it has sent acknowledgment    │

           │ > Consider a system essentially consisting of a sender S and │
           │ a receiver R that communicate with each other over channels  │
           │ c and d. The receiver sends messages to the sender via       │
           │ channel d, and the sender sends messages to the receiver via │
           │ channel c. It is assumed that both channels have an          │
           │ unlimited buffer. Channel c is unreliable in the sense that  │
           │ data may get lost when being transmitted from the sender S   │
           │ to channel c. Once messages are stored in the buffer of      │
           │ channel c, they are neither corrupted nor lost. Channel d is │
           │ assumed to be perfect. The goal is to design a communication │
           │ protocol that ensures any distinct transmitted datum by S to │
           │ be delivered to R. To ensure this in the presence of         │
           │ possible message losses, sender S resorts to                 │
           │ retransmissions. Messages are transmitted one by one, i.e.,  │
           │ S starts sending a new message once the transmission of the  │
           │ previous message has been successful. This is a simple flow  │
           │ control principle, known as "send-and-wait". Each message m  │
           │ from S is sent with a control bit b, and when R receives     │
           │ this, it sends an acknowledgement consisting of the control  │
           │ bit it just received. On receipt of the acknowledgement, S   │
           │ sends a new message with the negation of the control bit. If │
           │ S has to wait too long for the acknowledgement, it times out │
           │ and retransmits the original message and control bit. Build  │
           │ a model of this system, using submodules for S and R. Use    │
           │ the variable names c, d, S, R, m and b.                      │

           │ c and d. The receiver sends messages to the sender via       │
           │ channel d, and the sender sends messages to the receiver via │
           │ channel c. It is assumed that both channels have an          │
           │ unlimited buffer. Channel c is unreliable in the sense that  │
           │ data may get lost when being transmitted from the sender S   │
           │ to channel c. Once messages are stored in the buffer of      │
           │ channel c, they are neither corrupted nor lost. Channel d is │
           │ assumed to be perfect. The goal is to design a communication │
           │ protocol that ensures any distinct transmitted datum by S to │
           │ be delivered to R. To ensure this in the presence of         │
           │ possible message losses, sender S resorts to                 │
           │ retransmissions. Messages are transmitted one by one, i.e.,  │
           │ S starts sending a new message once the transmission of the  │
           │ previous message has been successful. This is a simple flow  │
           │ control principle, known as "send-and-wait". Each message m  │
           │ from S is sent with a control bit b, and when R receives     │
           │ this, it sends an acknowledgement consisting of the control  │
           │ bit it just received. On receipt of the acknowledgement, S   │
           │ sends a new message with the negation of the control bit. If │
           │ S has to wait too long for the acknowledgement, it times out │
           │ and retransmits the original message and control bit. Build  │
           │ a model of this system, using submodules for S and R. Use    │
           │ the variable names c, d, S, R, m and b.                      │
           │                                                              │
           │ Reply with your Python code inside one unique code block.    │
           │                                                              │

           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def instances(self):                                     │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the instances of other modules │
           │ and relates their                                            │
           │         input, output, and shared variables to local         │
           │ variables. Every instance                                    │

           │         implementation defines an instance of M called m,    │

           │             self.m = M(x=self.a, y=self.b, z=self.c)         │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │

           │                                                              │

           │         greater than or equal to 20:                         │
           │         ```                                                  │

           │         """                                                  │

           │                 self.x = self.x + 1                          │

           │         ```                                                  │

           │         pass                                                 │

           │                                                              │
           │ Each message m from S is sent with a control bit b, and when │
           │ R receives this, it sends an acknowledgement consisting of   │
           │ the control bit it just received. On receipt of the          │
           │ acknowledgement, S sends a new message with the negation of  │
           │ the control bit.  If S has to wait too long for the          │
           │ acknowledgement, it times out and retransmits the original   │
           │ message and control bit.                                     │
           │                                                              │
           │ Build a model of this system, using submodules for S and R.  │
           │                                                              │
           │ Use the variable names c, d, S, R, m and b                   │

           │                                                              │
           │ > Consider a system essentially consisting of a sender S and │
           │ a receiver R that communicate with each other over channels  │
           │ c and d. The receiver sends messages to the sender via       │
           │ channel d, and the sender sends messages to the receiver via │
           │ channel c. It is assumed that both channels have an          │
           │ unlimited buffer. Channel c is unreliable in the sense that  │
           │ data may get lost when being transmitted from the sender S   │
           │ to channel c. Once messages are stored in the buffer of      │
           │ channel c, they are neither corrupted nor lost. Channel d is │
           │ assumed to be perfect. The goal is to design a communication │
           │ protocol that ensures any distinct transmitted datum by S to │
           │ be delivered to R. To ensure this in the presence of         │
           │ possible message losses, sender S resorts to                 │
           │ retransmissions. Messages are transmitted one by one, i.e.,  │
           │ S starts sending a new message once the transmission of the  │
           │ previous message has been successful. This is a simple flow  │
           │ control principle, known as "send-and-wait". Each message m  │
           │ from S is sent with a control bit b, and when R receives     │
           │ this, it sends an acknowledgement consisting of the control  │
           │ bit it just received. On receipt of the acknowledgement, S   │
           │ sends a new message with the negation of the control bit. If │
           │ S has to wait too long for the acknowledgement, it times out │
           │ and retransmits the original message and control bit. Build  │
           │ a model of this system, using submodules for S and R. Use    │
           │ the variable names c, d, S, R, m and b.                      │
           │                                                              │
           │ Reply with your Python code inside one unique code block.    │
           │                                                              │
           │ ```python                                                    │
           │ class Module:                                                │
           │     """An abstract class to represent a UCLID5 module."""    │
           │                                                              │
           │     def types(self):                                         │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the type declarations.         │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines a  │
           │ 8-bit type called T:                                         │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def types(self):                                     │
           │             self.T = BitVector(8)                            │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def locals(self):                                        │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the local variables and their  │
           │ types.                                                       │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines an │
           │ 8-bit variable x                                             │
           │         and an integer variable y:                           │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def locals(self):                                    │
           │             self.x = BitVector(8)                            │
           │             self.y = Integer()                               │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def inputs(self):                                        │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the input variables and their  │
           │ types.                                                       │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines an │
           │ input variable x,                                            │
           │         which is an array of 8-bit bitvectors indexed by     │
           │ 2-bit bitvectors:                                            │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def inputs(self):                                    │
           │             self.x = Array(BitVector(2), BitVector(8))       │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def outputs(self):                                       │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the output variables and their │
           │ types.                                                       │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines an │
           │ output variable y,                                           │
           │         which is a real number:                              │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         def outputs(self):                                   │
           │             self.y = Real()                                  │
           │         ```                                                  │
           │         """                                                  │
           │         pass                                                 │
           │                                                              │
           │     def shared_vars(self):                                   │
           │         """(Optional) Defines the shared variables and their │
           │ types.                                                       │
           │         For example, the following implementation defines a  │
           │ shared variable z,                                           │

           │ > Consider a system essentially consisting of a sender S and │
           │ a receiver R that communicate with each other over channels  │
           │ c and d. The receiver sends messages to the sender via       │
           │ channel d, and the sender sends messages to the receiver via │
           │ channel c. It is assumed that both channels have an          │
           │ unlimited buffer. Channel c is unreliable in the sense that  │
           │ data may get lost when being transmitted from the sender S   │
           │ to channel c. Once messages are stored in the buffer of      │
           │ channel c, they are neither corrupted nor lost. Channel d is │
           │ assumed to be perfect. The goal is to design a communication │
           │ protocol that ensures any distinct transmitted datum by S to │
           │ be delivered to R. To ensure this in the presence of         │
           │ possible message losses, sender S resorts to                 │
           │ retransmissions. Messages are transmitted one by one, i.e.,  │
           │ S starts sending a new message once the transmission of the  │
           │ previous message has been successful. This is a simple flow  │
           │ control principle, known as "send-and-wait". Each message m  │
           │ from S is sent with a control bit b, and when R receives     │
           │ this, it sends an acknowledgement consisting of the control  │
           │ bit it just received. On receipt of the acknowledgement, S   │
           │ sends a new message with the negation of the control bit. If │
           │ S has to wait too long for the acknowledgement, it times out │
           │ and retransmits the original message and control bit. Build  │
           │ a model of this system, using submodules for S and R. Use    │
           │ the variable names c, d, S, R, m and b.                      │

           │ block: specification                                         │
           │ fix: The invariant S_inv currently asserts that if S equals  │
           │ m, then b should not equal b. This is logically incorrect    │
           │ and causes the verification failure as it implies a          │
           │ contradiction. Instead, S_inv should express the             │
           │ relationship between the control bit b's states during       │
           │ transmission-retransmission cycles. It ought to be amended   │
           │ to assert that if S broadcasts a message (i.e., S is True or │
           │ message in 'sent' state), the control bit should toggle      │
           │ after successful receipt acknowledgment by R.                │
           │                                                              │
           │ block: specification                                         │
           │ fix: The c_inv invariant currently asserts that c should     │
           │ always be different from d. This is incorrect as c is a      │
           │ buffer channel used by the sender to transmit data, and d is │
           │ a perfect channel used by the receiver for acknowledgments.  │
           │ The criterion for c_inv should validate that if the sender   │
           │ is in transmission phase, c should reflect the state waiting │
           │ for acknowledgment or retransmission and align with data     │
           │ transmission logic, not that it remains always different     │
           │ from d.                                                      │
           │                                                              │
           │ block: specification                                         │
           │ fix: The m_inv invariant states that R should always be      │
           │ equal to m. This does not represent the specified relation   │
           │ accurately as it does not take into account the toggling     │
           │ mechanism of b. The correct invariant should confirm that if │
           │ a message is sent and subsequently acknowledged, R should    │
           │ reflect the acknowledged message state aligned with d's      │
           │ acknowledgment holding rather than m directly.               │

           │     #TODO: The m_inv invariant states that R should always   │
